A system-scoped dependency is configured\n2. The file doesn't exist at the specified systemPath\n3. Common with tools.jar on Java 9+\n\n## Resolution Strategies\n\n1. **Update dependency versions** to ones compatible with current Java version\n2. **Remove obsolete dependencies** (e.g., tools.jar not needed in Java 9+)\n3. **Use profiles** to conditionally include dependencies based on Java version\n4. **Override parent POM settings** in child projects\n5. **Exclude transitive dependencies** that cause conflicts\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":118,"cache_read_input_tokens":51711,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":118,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"2ecafc1b-c71b-44cc-8aaf-64867ac193af","uuid":"1685bcf2-cac5-43f9-b153-a180197b3b9b"}

Java Version Compatibility\n\n## Overview\nJava has evolved significantly across major versions, with changes that affect build configurations and dependencies.\n\n## Java 9+ Module System Changes\n\n### The tools.jar File\n- **Java 8 and earlier**: Compiler classes (javac) were in `JAVA_HOME/lib/tools.jar`\n- **Java 9+**: Classes moved into modules, tools.jar no longer exists\n- The compiler classes are now in `java.compiler` and `jdk.compiler` modules\n\n### Impact on Maven Builds\nProjects that depend on tools.jar via system-scoped dependencies will fail on Java 9+:\n\n```xml\n<!-- This fails on Java 9+ -->\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>com.sun</groupId>\n  <artifactId>tools</artifactId>\n  <version>${java.version}</version>\n  <scope>system</scope>\n  <systemPath>${java.home}/../lib/tools.jar</systemPath>\n</dependency>\n```\n\n## Fixing tools.jar Dependencies\n\n### Option 1: Remove the Dependency\nIf the code doesn't actually use tools.jar classes, exclude it:\n\n```xml\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>com.google.testing.compile</groupId>\n  <artifactId>compile-testing</artifactId>\n  <version>0.6</version>\n  <scope>test</scope>\n  <exclusions>\n    <exclusion>\n      <groupId>com.sun</groupId>\n      <artifactId>tools</artifactId>\n    </exclusion>\n  </exclusions>\n</dependency>\n```\n\n### Option 2: Update to Newer Versions\nNewer versions of libraries often remove tools.jar dependencies:\n\n```xml\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>com.google.testing.compile</groupId>\n  <artifactId>compile-testing</artifactId>\n  <version>0.18</version> <!-- Updated version -->\n  <scope>test</scope>\n</dependency>\n```\n\n### Option 3: Use Java Version Profiles\nConditionally exclude based on Java version:\n\n```xml\n<profiles>\n  <profile>\n    <id>java9-and-above</id>\n    <activation>\n      <jdk>[9,)</jdk>\n    </activation>\n    <build>\n      <plugins>\n        <plugin>\n          <groupId>org.apache.maven.plugins</groupId>\n          <artifactId>maven-compiler-plugin</artifactId>\n          <configuration>\n            <release>11</release>\n          </configuration>\n        </plugin>\n      </plugins>\n    </build>\n  </profile>\n</profiles>\n```\n\n## Java Version Detection\n\n### Check Current Java Version\n```bash\njava -version\njavac -version\n```\n\n### Verify JAVA_HOME\n```bash\necho $JAVA_HOME\nls -la $JAVA_HOME/lib/\n```\n\nIn Java 11, `$JAVA_HOME/lib/` won't contain tools.jar.\n\n## Source/Target Compatibility\n\n### Legacy Configuration (pre-Java 9)\n```xml\n<configuration>\n  <source>1.6</source>\n  <target>1.6</target>\n</configuration>\n```\n\n### Modern Configuration (Java 9+)\n```xml\n<configuration>\n  <release>11</release>\n</configuration>\n```\n\nThe `release` flag ensures consistent compilation across Java versions.\n\n## Common Compatibility Issues\n\n1. **tools.jar missing** - Java 9+ removed this file\n2. **Deprecated APIs removed** - Code using removed APIs fails to compile\n3. **Module system requirements** - Some libraries require module declarations\n4. **Bytecode compatibility** - Classes compiled with newer Java may not run on older JVMs\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Test builds on target Java version** before deployment\n2. **Update dependencies regularly** to versions supporting newer Java\n3. **Use dependency exclusions** sparingly and document why\n4. **Set explicit source/target** or release versions in compiler plugin\n5. **Use Maven Enforcer plugin** to ensure consistent Java versions\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":750,"cache_read_input_tokens":51829,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":750,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":25},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"2ecafc1b-c71b-44cc-8aaf-64867ac193af","uuid":"cc6fd327-d84e-4564-97bd-8521ead3fa06"}

It's an XML file (pom.xml) that contains project and configuration information.\n\n## Basic POM Structure\n\n```xml\n<project xmlns=\"http://maven.apache.org/POM/4.0.0\"\n         xmlns:xsi=\"http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance\"\n         xsi:schemaLocation=\"http://maven.apache.org/POM/4.0.0\n         http://maven.apache.org/maven-v4_0_0.xsd\">\n  <modelVersion>4.0.0</modelVersion>\n\n  <groupId>com.example</groupId>\n  <artifactId>my-project</artifactId>\n  <version>1.0-SNAPSHOT</version>\n  <packaging>jar</packaging>\n\n  <name>My Project</name>\n  <description>Project description</description>\n\n  <properties>\n    <project.build.sourceEncoding>UTF-8</project.build.sourceEncoding>\n    <maven.compiler.source>11</maven.compiler.source>\n    <maven.compiler.target>11</maven.compiler.target>\n  </properties>\n\n  <dependencies>\n    <!-- Dependencies here -->\n  </dependencies>\n\n  <build>\n    <plugins>\n      <!-- Build plugins here -->\n    </plugins>\n  </build>\n</project>\n```\n\n## POM Inheritance\n\n### Parent POM\nProjects can inherit from a parent POM:\n\n```xml\n<parent>\n  <groupId>com.example</groupId>\n  <artifactId>parent-pom</artifactId>\n  <version>1.0</version>\n</parent>\n```\n\nChild POMs inherit:\n- Dependencies (from dependencyManagement)\n- Plugins (from pluginManagement)\n- Properties\n- Build configuration\n\n### Overriding Parent Settings\nChild POMs can override parent settings by redefining them.\n\n## Dependencies\n\n### Basic Dependency\n```xml\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>org.example</groupId>\n  <artifactId>example-library</artifactId>\n  <version>1.2.3</version>\n</dependency>\n```\n\n### Dependency Scopes\n- `compile` (default): Available in all classpaths\n- `provided`: Expected to be provided by JDK or container\n- `runtime`: Not needed for compilation, only for execution\n- `test`: Only for testing\n- `system`: Must provide explicit path (not recommended)\n\n### System-Scoped Dependency\n```xml\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>com.sun</groupId>\n  <artifactId>tools</artifactId>\n  <version>${java.version}</version>\n  <scope>system</scope>\n  <systemPath>${java.home}/../lib/tools.jar</systemPath>\n</dependency>\n```\n\nSystem scope issues:\n- Not portable\n- Path may not exist\n- Not recommended in modern Maven\n\n### Excluding Transitive Dependencies\n```xml\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>com.example</groupId>\n  <artifactId>example-lib</artifactId>\n  <version>1.0</version>\n  <exclusions>\n    <exclusion>\n      <groupId>com.unwanted</groupId>\n      <artifactId>unwanted-dependency</artifactId>\n    </exclusion>\n  </exclusions>\n</dependency>\n```\n\n## Properties\n\nProperties allow parameterization and reuse:\n\n```xml\n<properties>\n  <java.version>11</java.version>\n  <guava.version>30.1-jre</guava.version>\n  <toolsjar>${java.home}/../lib/tools.jar</toolsjar>\n</properties>\n```\n\nUse properties with `${property.name}` syntax.\n\n### Built-in Properties\n- `${project.version}` - Current project version\n- `${project.groupId}` - Current project groupId\n- `${java.version}` - Java version\n- `${java.home}` - Java installation directory\n- `${maven.build.timestamp}` - Build timestamp\n\n## Build Configuration\n\n### Compiler Plugin\n```xml\n<build>\n  <plugins>\n    <plugin>\n      <groupId>org.apache.maven.plugins</groupId>\n      <artifactId>maven-compiler-plugin</artifactId>\n      <version>3.8.1</version>\n      <configuration>\n        <source>11</source>\n        <target>11</target>\n      </configuration>\n    </plugin>\n  </plugins>\n</build>\n```\n\n### Plugin Configuration Overriding\nChild POMs can override plugin configuration from parent:\n\n```xml\n<!-- Parent POM -->\n<plugin>\n  <artifactId>maven-compiler-plugin</artifactId>\n  <configuration>\n    <source>1.6</source>\n    <target>1.6</target>\n  </configuration>\n</plugin>\n\n<!-- Child POM - override without losing parent config -->\n<plugin>\n  <artifactId>maven-compiler-plugin</artifactId>\n  <configuration>\n    <!-- Adds to parent config -->\n    <compilerArgument>-proc:none</compilerArgument>\n  </configuration>\n</plugin>\n```\n\n## Profiles\n\nProfiles allow conditional configuration:\n\n```xml\n<profiles>\n  <profile>\n    <id>java8-profile</id>\n    <activation>\n      <jdk>1.8</jdk>\n    </activation>\n    <properties>\n      <toolsjar>${java.home}/../lib/tools.jar</toolsjar>\n    </properties>\n  </profile>\n\n  <profile>\n    <id>java9-plus</id>\n    <activation>\n      <jdk>[9,)</jdk>\n    </activation>\n    <dependencies>\n      <!-- Different dependencies for Java 9+ -->\n    </dependencies>\n  </profile>\n</profiles>\n```\n\n### Profile Activation\n- By JDK version: `<jdk>11</jdk>` or `<jdk>[9,)</jdk>`\n- By OS: `<os><family>unix</family></os>`\n- By property: `<property><name>env</name><value>prod</value></property>`\n- By file existence: `<file><exists>path/to/file</exists></file>`\n- Explicitly: `mvn clean install -P profile-id`\n\n## Dependency Management\n\nIn parent POMs, use `<dependencyManagement>` to declare versions without adding dependencies:\n\n```xml\n<dependencyManagement>\n  <dependencies>\n    <dependency>\n      <groupId>com.google.guava</groupId>\n      <artifactId>guava</artifactId>\n      <version>30.1-jre</version>\n    </dependency>\n  </dependencies>\n</dependencyManagement>\n```\n\nChild POMs can then omit version:\n\n```xml\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>com.google.guava</groupId>\n  <artifactId>guava</artifactId>\n  <!-- version inherited from parent -->\n</dependency>\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Use properties** for versions and repeated values\n2. **Avoid system-scoped dependencies** - use repository dependencies instead\n3. **Use dependencyManagement** in parent POMs for version control\n4. **Exclude carefully** - understand transitive dependency trees first\n5. **Document overrides** - comment why parent settings are changed\n6. **Use profiles sparingly** - complexity increases with profiles\n7. **Keep POMs DRY** - use parent POMs to avoid duplication\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1087,"cache_read_input_tokens":52579,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1087,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"2ecafc1b-c71b-44cc-8aaf-64867ac193af","uuid":"bc5333a6-bdd1-458e-9fca-484a9bc4cde5"}

It's an XML file (pom.xml) that contains project and configuration information.\n\n## Basic POM Structure\n\n```xml\n<project xmlns=\"http://maven.apache.org/POM/4.0.0\"\n         xmlns:xsi=\"http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema-instance\"\n         xsi:schemaLocation=\"http://maven.apache.org/POM/4.0.0\n         http://maven.apache.org/maven-v4_0_0.xsd\">\n <maven.compiler.source>11</maven.compiler.source>\n    <maven.compiler.target>11</maven.compiler.target>\n  </properties>\n\n  <dependencies>\n    <!-- Dependencies here -->\n  </dependencies>\n\n  <build>\n    <plugins>\n      <!-- Build plugins here -->\n    </plugins>\n  </build>\n</project>\n```\n\n## POM Inheritance\n\n### Parent POM\nProjects can inherit from a parent POM:\n\n```xml\n<parent>\n  <groupId>com.example</groupId>\n  <artifactId>parent-pom</artifactId>\n  <version>1.0</version>\n</parent>\n```\n\nChild POMs inherit:\n- Dependencies (from dependencyManagement)\n- Plugins (from pluginManagement)\n- Properties\n- Build configuration\n\n### Overriding Parent Settings\nChild POMs can override parent settings by redefining them.\n\n## Dependencies\n\n### Basic Dependency\n```xml\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>org.example</groupId>\n  <artifactId>example-library</artifactId>\n  <version>1.2.3</version>\n</dependency>\n```\n\n### Dependency Scopes\n- `compile` (default): Available in all classpaths\n- `provided`: Expected to be provided by JDK or container\n- `runtime`: Not needed for compilation, only for execution\n- `test`: Only for testing\n- `system`: Must provide explicit path (not recommended)\n\n### System-Scoped Dependency\n```xml\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>com.sun</groupId>\n  <artifactId>tools</artifactId>\n  <version>${java.version}</version>\n  <scope>system</scope>\n  <systemPath>${java.home}/../lib/tools.jar</systemPath>\n</dependency>\n```\n\nSystem scope issues:\n- Not portable\n- Path may not exist\n- Not recommended in modern Maven\n\n### Excluding Transitive Dependencies\n```xml\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>com.example</groupId>\n  <artifactId>example-lib</artifactId>\n  <version>1.0</version>\n  <exclusions>\n    <exclusion>\n      <groupId>com.unwanted</groupId>\n      <artifactId>unwanted-dependency</artifactId>\n    </exclusion>\n  </exclusions>\n</dependency>\n```\n\n## Properties\n\nProperties allow parameterization and reuse:\n\n```xml\n<properties>\n  <java.version>11</java.version>\n  <guava.version>30.1-jre</guava.version>\n  <toolsjar>${java.home}/../lib/tools.jar</toolsjar>\n</properties>\n```\n\nUse properties with `${property.name}` syntax.\n\n### Built-in Properties\n- `${project.version}` - Current project version\n- `${project.groupId}` - Current project groupId\n- `${java.version}` - Java version\n- `${java.home}` - Java installation directory\n- `${maven.build.timestamp}` - Build timestamp\n\n## Build Configuration\n\n### Compiler Plugin\n```xml\n<build>\n  <plugins>\n    <plugin>\n      <groupId>org.apache.maven.plugins</groupId>\n      <artifactId>maven-compiler-plugin</artifactId>\n      <version>3.8.1</version>\n      <configuration>\n        <source>11</source>\n        <target>11</target>\n      </configuration>\n    </plugin>\n  </plugins>\n</build>\n```\n\n### Plugin Configuration Overriding\nChild POMs can override plugin configuration from parent:\n\n```xml\n<!-- Parent POM -->\n<plugin>\n  <artifactId>maven-compiler-plugin</artifactId>\n  <configuration>\n    <source>1.6</source>\n    <target>1.6</target>\n  </configuration>\n</plugin>\n\n<!-- Child POM - override without losing parent config -->\n<plugin>\n  <artifactId>maven-compiler-plugin</artifactId>\n  <configuration>\n    <!-- Adds to parent config -->\n    <compilerArgument>-proc:none</compilerArgument>\n  </configuration>\n</plugin>\n```\n\n## Profiles\n\nProfiles allow conditional configuration:\n\n```xml\n<profiles>\n  <profile>\n    <id>java8-profile</id>\n    <activation>\n      <jdk>1.8</jdk>\n    </activation>\n    <properties>\n      <toolsjar>${java.home}/../lib/tools.jar</toolsjar>\n    </properties>\n  </profile>\n\n  <profile>\n    <id>java9-plus</id>\n    <activation>\n      <jdk>[9,)</jdk>\n    </activation>\n    <dependencies>\n      <!-- Different dependencies for Java 9+ -->\n    </dependencies>\n  </profile>\n</profiles>\n```\n\n### Profile Activation\n- By JDK version: `<jdk>11</jdk>` or `<jdk>[9,)</jdk>`\n- By OS: `<os><family>unix</family></os>`\n- By property: `<property><name>env</name><value>prod</value></property>`\n- By file existence: `<file><exists>path/to/file</exists></file>`\n- Explicitly: `mvn clean install -P profile-id`\n\n## Dependency Management\n\nIn parent POMs, use `<dependencyManagement>` to declare versions without adding dependencies:\n\n```xml\n<dependencyManagement>\n  <dependencies>\n    <dependency>\n      <groupId>com.google.guava</groupId>\n      <artifactId>guava</artifactId>\n      <version>30.1-jre</version>\n    </dependency>\n  </dependencies>\n</dependencyManagement>\n```\n\nChild POMs can then omit version:\n\n```xml\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>com.google.guava</groupId>\n  <artifactId>guava</artifactId>\n  <!-- version inherited from parent -->\n</dependency>\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Use properties** for versions and repeated values\n2. **Avoid system-scoped dependencies** - use repository dependencies instead\n3. **Use dependencyManagement** in parent POMs for version control\n4. **Exclude carefully** - understand transitive dependency trees first\n5. **Document overrides** - comment why parent settings are changed\n6. **Use profiles sparingly** - complexity increases with profiles\n7. **Keep POMs DRY** - use parent POMs to avoid duplication\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

They're used for version control, code review, and distributing changes.\n\n## Standard Diff Formats\n\n### Unified Diff Format\nThe most common format, used by Git and modern tools:\n\n```diff\n--- a/path/to/original/file.txt\n+++ b/path/to/modified/file.txt\n@@ -10,7 +10,8 @@\n context line\n context line\n-removed line\n+added line\n+another added line\n context line\n context line\n```\n\n### Components\n\n1. **File headers**: Indicate which file is being modified\n   ```diff\n   --- a/original/path/to/file.xml\n   +++ b/modified/path/to/file.xml\n   ```\n\n2. **Hunk headers**: Show location and size of changes\n   ```diff\n   @@ -10,7 +10,8 @@\n   ```\n   Format: `@@ -start_line,num_lines +start_line,num_lines @@`\n   - First pair: original file (line 10, 7 lines)\n   - Second pair: modified file (line 10, 8 lines)\n\n3. **Change lines**:\n   - Lines starting with `-` are removed\n   - Lines starting with `+` are added\n   - Lines starting with ` ` (space) are context (unchanged)\n\n## Creating Patch Files Manually\n\n### Basic Structure\n```diff\n--- a/path/to/file.ext\n+++ b/path/to/file.ext\n@@ -line_num,count +line_num,count @@\n context\n-old content\n+new content\n context\n```\n\n### Example: XML File Patch\n\n```diff\n--- a/pom.xml\n+++ b/pom.xml\n@@ -42,6 +42,14 @@\n   <dependencies>\n     <dependency>\n       <groupId>com.google.auto</groupId>\n       <artifactId>auto-common</artifactId>\n       <version>0.3</version>\n+      <exclusions>\n+        <exclusion>\n+          <groupId>com.sun</groupId>\n+          <artifactId>tools</artifactId>\n+        </exclusion>\n+      </exclusions>\n     </dependency>\n     <dependency>\n       <groupId>com.google.guava</groupId>\n```\n\n## Rules for Valid Diff Files\n\n1. **Context lines are crucial**: Include 3+ lines of context before and after changes\n2. **Exact matching**: Context lines must match exactly (including whitespace)\n3. **Line endings**: Be consistent with line endings (LF vs CRLF)\n4. **Indentation**: Preserve exact indentation from original file\n\n## Creating Diffs with Git\n\n### Single File Diff\n```bash\ngit diff path/to/file > changes.patch\n```\n\n### Diff Between Commits\n```bash\ngit diff commit1 commit2 > changes.patch\n```\n\n### Create Diff Without Git Repository\n```bash\ndiff -u original.txt modified.txt > changes.patch\n```\n\n## Applying Patch Files\n\n### Using patch command\n```bash\npatch -p1 < changes.patch\n```\n\nThe `-p1` flag strips the first directory level from paths (removes `a/` and `b/`).\n\n### Using Git\n```bash\ngit apply changes.patch\n```\n\n### Dry run (test without applying)\n```bash\npatch --dry-run -p1 < changes.patch\n# or\ngit apply --check changes.patch\n```\n\n## Common Pitfalls\n\n1. **Wrong context**: If context doesn't match, patch fails\n   ```\n   Solution: Ensure context lines match the actual file\n   ```\n\n2. **Line ending mismatches**: Windows (CRLF) vs Unix (LF)\n   ```\n   Solution: Normalize line endings before creating patch\n   ```\n\n3. **Tab vs space confusion**: Especially in indentation\n   ```\n   Solution: Use exact whitespace from original file\n   ```\n\n4. **Path issues**: Incorrect file paths in headers\n   ```\n   Solution: Use paths relative to repository root\n   ```\n\n## Multiple File Patches\n\nA single patch file can contain changes to multiple files:\n\n```diff\n--- a/file1.txt\n+++ b/file1.txt\n@@ -1,3 +1,3 @@\n line 1\n-old line 2\n+new line 2\n line 3\n--- a/file2.txt\n+++ b/file2.txt\n@@ -5,3 +5,4 @@\n context\n context\n+added line\n context\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Include sufficient context** (3-5 lines) for reliable patching\n2. **Use meaningful filenames** for patch files (e.g., `fix-tools-jar-dependency.patch`)\n3. **Test patches** before distributing (use --dry-run)\n4. **Document patches** with comments about what they fix\n5. **Keep patches focused** - one logical change per patch file\n6. **Use relative paths** from repository root\n7. **Preserve whitespace** exactly as in original\n\n## Naming Conventions\n\n- `patch_1.diff` - Sequential numbering\n- `fix-dependency-issue.patch` - Descriptive names\n- `001-update-pom.diff` - Numbered with description\n\n## Verification\n\nAfter creating a patch, verify it:\n```bash\n# Check patch syntax\npatch --dry-run -p1 < patch_file.diff\n\n# View what would change\ngit apply --stat patch_file.diff\n\n# Check if it applies cleanly\ngit apply --check patch_file.diff\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1970,"cache_read_input_tokens":53666,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1970,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"2ecafc1b-c71b-44cc-8aaf-64867ac193af","uuid":"072088e3-bf29-4fe8-9e50-e1e61c7b78a1"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929","id":"msg_vrtx_01KbnucJ1ieTdLHoRf5UCepC","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01ApT7tmufp7aXfPJ8YoKgqg","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/home/travis/environment/skills/annotation-processors-java.md","content":"# Java Annotation Processors\n\n## Overview\nAnnotation processors are compiler plugins that process annotations at compile-time to generate code, validate constraints, or produce additional resources.\n\n## How Annotation Processors Work\n\n### Compile-Time Processing\n1. Compiler scans source code for annotations\n2. Calls registered annotation processors\n3. Processors can:\n   - Analyze annotated elements\n   - Generate new source files\n   - Generate resources\n   - Report errors/warnings\n\n### Example Annotation\n```java\n@AutoService(MyInterface.class)\npublic class MyImplementation implements MyInterface {\n    // Implementation\n}\n```\n\nThe `@AutoService` annotation processor automatically generates the service provider configuration file.\n\n## Common Use Cases\n\n### 1. Code Generation\nGenerate boilerplate code automatically:\n- `@AutoValue` - generates value classes\n- `@AutoFactory` - generates factory classes\n- `@AutoService` - generates service provider configs\n\n### 2. Validation\nEnforce constraints at compile-time:\n- Check naming conventions\n- Validate annotations combinations\n- Ensure required methods exist\n\n### 3. Configuration\nGenerate metadata files:\n- Service provider files (META-INF/services/*)\n- Dependency injection configs\n- JSON schemas\n\n## ServiceLoader Pattern\n\n### META-INF/services Files\nService provider mechanism uses files in `META-INF/services/`:\n\n```\nMETA-INF/services/com.example.MyInterface\n```\n\nContent (one implementation per line):\n```\ncom.example.impl.MyImplementationOne\ncom.example.impl.MyImplementationTwo\n```\n\n### Loading Services\n```java\nServiceLoader<MyInterface> loader = ServiceLoader.load(MyInterface.class);\nfor (MyInterface service : loader) {\n    // Use service\n}\n```\n\n## @AutoService Annotation\n\n### Purpose\nAutomatically generates service provider configuration files.\n\n### Usage\n```java\nimport com.google.auto.service.AutoService;\n\n@AutoService(MyInterface.class)\npublic class MyImpl implements MyInterface {\n    // ...\n}\n```\n\n### Generated File\nThe processor creates:\n```\nMETA-INF/services/com.example.MyInterface\n```\n\nContaining:\n```\ncom.example.MyImpl\n```\n\n## Annotation Processor Dependencies\n\n### Compile vs Runtime\nAnnotation processors are typically needed at compile-time only:\n\n```xml\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>com.google.auto.service</groupId>\n  <artifactId>auto-service</artifactId>\n  <version>1.0</version>\n  <scope>provided</scope> <!-- or <optional>true</optional> -->\n</dependency>\n```\n\n### Processor Path\nSome build tools require explicit processor path configuration:\n\n```xml\n<plugin>\n  <artifactId>maven-compiler-plugin</artifactId>\n  <configuration>\n    <annotationProcessorPaths>\n      <path>\n        <groupId>com.google.auto.service</groupId>\n        <artifactId>auto-service</artifactId>\n        <version>1.0</version>\n      </path>\n    </annotationProcessorPaths>\n  </configuration>\n</plugin>\n```\n\n## Disabling Annotation Processing\n\n### Maven Compiler Plugin\n```xml\n<plugin>\n  <artifactId>maven-compiler-plugin</artifactId>\n  <configuration>\n    <compilerArgument>-proc:none</compilerArgument>\n  </configuration>\n</plugin>\n```\n\nOptions:\n- `-proc:none` - No processing\n- `-proc:only` - Only process, don't compile\n- Default - Process and compile\n\n### Why Disable?\n- Bootstrapping processors (compiling the processor itself)\n- Avoiding circular dependencies\n- Build performance\n- Testing without side effects\n\n## Tools for Testing Processors\n\n### compile-testing Library\nGoogle's library for testing annotation processors:\n\n```xml\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>com.google.testing.compile</groupId>\n  <artifactId>compile-testing</artifactId>\n  <version>0.18</version>\n  <scope>test</scope>\n</dependency>\n```\n\n### Example Test\n```java\n@Test\npublic void testProcessor() {\n    JavaFileObject source = JavaFileObjects.forSourceString(\n        \"com.example.Test\",\n        \"package com.example;\\n\" +\n        \"@AutoService(Runnable.class)\\n\" +\n        \"public class Test implements Runnable {\\n\" +\n        \"  public void run() {}\\n\" +\n        \"}\"\n    );\n\n    Compilation compilation = Compiler.javac()\n        .withProcessors(new AutoServiceProcessor())\n        .compile(source);\n\n    assertThat(compilation).succeeded();\n    assertThat(compilation)\n        .generatedFile(StandardLocation.CLASS_OUTPUT,\n            \"META-INF/services/java.lang.Runnable\")\n        .contentsAsUtf8String()\n        .contains(\"com.example.Test\");\n}\n```\n\n## Common Issues\n\n### 1. Processor Not Found\n```\nError: Annotation processor 'com.example.MyProcessor' not found\n```\n\nSolution: Ensure processor is on classpath or in annotationProcessorPaths\n\n### 2. Circular Dependencies\n```\nError: Compilation failed; processor generated files that reference itself\n```\n\nSolution: Use `-proc:none` when compiling the processor itself\n\n### 3. Missing tools.jar (Java 8 and earlier)\n```\nError: Could not find tools.jar\n```\n\nSolution: Ensure JAVA_HOME points to JDK (not JRE)\n\n### 4. Java 9+ Module Issues\nIn Java 9+, compiler classes are in modules. Older processor libraries may fail.\n\nSolution: Update to newer versions or add module dependencies\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Use provided/optional scope** for processor dependencies\n2. **Test processors** with compile-testing library\n3. **Handle errors gracefully** - report useful error messages\n4. **Generate deterministic output** - same input → same output\n5. **Don't modify existing files** - only generate new ones\n6. **Document generated code** with source attribution\n7. **Support incremental compilation** when possible\n\n## Maven Configuration Example\n\nComplete processor setup:\n\n```xml\n<dependencies>\n  <!-- Annotation (needed at compile time) -->\n  <dependency>\n    <groupId>com.google.auto.service</groupId>\n    <artifactId>auto-service-annotations</artifactId>\n    <version>1.0</version>\n  </dependency>\n\n  <!-- Processor (provided scope) -->\n  <dependency>\n    <groupId>com.google.auto.service</groupId>\n    <artifactId>auto-service</artifactId>\n    <version>1.0</version>\n    <scope>provided</scope>\n  </dependency>\n</dependencies>\n\n<build>\n  <plugins>\n    <plugin>\n      <artifactId>maven-compiler-plugin</artifactId>\n      <version>3.8.1</version>\n      <configuration>\n        <!-- Let processor run automatically -->\n        <annotationProcessorPaths>\n          <path>\n            <groupId>com.google.auto.service</groupId>\n            <artifactId>auto-service</artifactId>\n            <version>1.0</version>\n          </path>\n        </annotationProcessorPaths>\n      </configuration>\n    </plugin>\n  </plugins>\n</build>\n```\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":0,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1500,"cache_read_input_tokens":55636,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1500,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"2ecafc1b-c71b-44cc-8aaf-64867ac193af","uuid":"74c1e167-5010-40cb-a3ac-772e2af5ca4b"}

line):\n```\ncom.example.impl.MyImplementationOne\ncom.example.impl.MyImplementationTwo\n```\n\n### Loading Services\n```java\nServiceLoader<MyInterface> loader = ServiceLoader.load(MyInterface.class);\nfor (MyInterface service : loader) {\n    // Use service\n}\n```\n\n## @AutoService Annotation\n\n### Purpose\nAutomatically generates service provider configuration files.\n\n### Usage\n```java\nimport com.google.auto.service.AutoService;\n\n@AutoService(MyInterface.class)\npublic class MyImpl implements MyInterface {\n    // ...\n}\n```\n\n### Generated File\nThe processor creates:\n```\nMETA-INF/services/com.example.MyInterface\n```\n\nContaining:\n```\ncom.example.MyImpl\n```\n\n## Annotation Processor Dependencies\n\n### Compile vs Runtime\nAnnotation processors are typically needed at compile-time only:\n\n```xml\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>com.google.auto.service</groupId>\n  <artifactId>auto-service</artifactId>\n  <version>1.0</version>\n  <scope>provided</scope> <!-- or <optional>true</optional> -->\n</dependency>\n```\n\n### Processor Path\nSome build tools require explicit processor path configuration:\n\n```xml\n<plugin>\n  <artifactId>maven-compiler-plugin</artifactId>\n  <configuration>\n    <annotationProcessorPaths>\n      <path>\n        <groupId>com.google.auto.service</groupId>\n        <artifactId>auto-service</artifactId>\n        <version>1.0</version>\n      </path>\n    </annotationProcessorPaths>\n  </configuration>\n</plugin>\n```\n\n## Disabling Annotation Processing\n\n### Maven Compiler Plugin\n```xml\n<plugin>\n  <artifactId>maven-compiler-plugin</artifactId>\n  <configuration>\n    <compilerArgument>-proc:none</compilerArgument>\n  </configuration>\n</plugin>\n```\n\nOptions:\n- `-proc:none` - No processing\n- `-proc:only` - Only process, don't compile\n- Default - Process and compile\n\n### Why Disable?\n- Bootstrapping processors (compiling the processor itself)\n- Avoiding circular dependencies\n- Build performance\n- Testing without side effects\n\n## Tools for Testing Processors\n\n### compile-testing Library\nGoogle's library for testing annotation processors:\n\n```xml\n<dependency>\n  <groupId>com.google.testing.compile</groupId>\n  <artifactId>compile-testing</artifactId>\n  <version>0.18</version>\n  <scope>test</scope>\n</dependency>\n```\n\n### Example Test\n```java\n@Test\npublic void testProcessor() {\n    JavaFileObject source = JavaFileObjects.forSourceString(\n        \"com.example.Test\",\n        \"package com.example;\\n\" +\n        \"@AutoService(Runnable.class)\\n\" +\n        \"public class Test implements Runnable {\\n\" +\n        \"  public void run() {}\\n\" +\n        \"}\"\n    );\n\n    Compilation compilation = Compiler.javac()\n        .withProcessors(new AutoServiceProcessor())\n        .compile(source);\n\n    assertThat(compilation).succeeded();\n    assertThat(compilation)\n        .generatedFile(StandardLocation.CLASS_OUTPUT,\n            \"META-INF/services/java.lang.Runnable\")\n        .contentsAsUtf8String()\n        .contains(\"com.example.Test\");\n}\n```\n\n## Common Issues\n\n### 1. Processor Not Found\n```\nError: Annotation processor 'com.example.MyProcessor' not found\n```\n\nSolution: Ensure processor is on classpath or in annotationProcessorPaths\n\n### 2. Circular Dependencies\n```\nError: Compilation failed; processor generated files that reference itself\n```\n\nSolution: Use `-proc:none` when compiling the processor itself\n\n### 3. Missing tools.jar (Java 8 and earlier)\n```\nError: Could not find tools.jar\n```\n\nSolution: Ensure JAVA_HOME points to JDK (not JRE)\n\n### 4. Java 9+ Module Issues\nIn Java 9+, compiler classes are in modules. Older processor libraries may fail.\n\nSolution: Update to newer versions or add module dependencies\n\n## Best Practices\n\n1. **Use provided/optional scope** for processor dependencies\n2. **Test processors** with compile-testing library\n3. **Handle errors gracefully** - report useful error messages\n4. **Generate deterministic output** - same input → same output\n5. **Don't modify existing files** - only generate new ones\n6. **Document generated code** with source attribution\n7. **Support incremental compilation** when possible\n\n## Maven Configuration Example\n\nComplete processor setup:\n\n```xml\n<dependencies>\n  <!-- Annotation (needed at compile time) -->\n  <dependency>\n    <groupId>com.google.auto.service</groupId>\n    <artifactId>auto-service-annotations</artifactId>\n    <version>1.0</version>\n  </dependency>\n\n  <!-- Processor (provided scope) -->\n  <dependency>\n    <groupId>com.google.auto.service</groupId>\n    <artifactId>auto-service</artifactId>\n    <version>1.0</version>\n    <scope>provided</scope>\n  </dependency>\n</dependencies>\n\n<build>\n  <plugins>\n    <plugin>\n      <artifactId>maven-compiler-plugin</artifactId>\n      <version>3.8.1</version>\n      <configuration>\n        <!-- Let processor run automatically -->\n        <annotationProcessorPaths>\n          <path>\n            <groupId>com.google.auto.service</groupId>\n            <artifactId>auto-service</artifactId>\n            <version>1.0</version>\n          </path>\n        </annotationProcessorPaths>\n      </configuration>\n    </plugin>\n  </plugins>\n</build>\n```\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

WHY tools.jar IS MISSING:\n   - In Java 8 and earlier: Compiler classes (javac, etc.) were packaged in JAVA_HOME/lib/tools.jar\n   - In Java 9+: The module system was introduced, and these classes were moved to modules\n   - The tools.jar file was removed entirely starting with Java 9\n   - The classes are now in jdk.compiler and java.compiler modules\n\n3. Maintains compatibility with Java 11\n   - No version changes required\n\n   Cons:\n   - If tests actually use tools.jar classes directly, they might fail\n   - Need to verify tests still pass\n\nSOLUTION 2: Update compile-testing to a newer version\n   - Newer versions (0.18+) have removed the tools.jar dependency\n   - They work correctly with Java 9+\n\n   Implementation:\n   Update compile-testing version from 0.6 to 0.18 or later\n\n   Pros:\n   - More future-proof\n   - Better Java 9+ support\n   - Cleaner solution\n\n   Cons:\n   - May require API changes in test code\n   - Higher risk of breaking existing tests\n   - Not chosen for this fix due to potential test incompatibilities\n\nSOLUTION 3: Add Java version profile to exclude on Java 9+\n   -
